## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the issue description:

1.1 Code/config snippets analysis:
- The user runs `reportseff -u $U` and gets proper MemEff values (72.2%, 74.5%)
- When running `reportseff -u $U -s TIMEOUT`, MemEff becomes 0.0% for TIMEOUT jobs

1.2 Technical details:
- Version mentioned: 2.7.6
- Issue affects MemEff calculation specifically
- The problem occurs when filtering by state (-s TIMEOUT)
- Other metrics (TimeEff, CPUEff) remain correct
- Only TIMEOUT jobs are affected

1.3 Problem explanation:
The memory efficiency calculation is incorrectly returning 0% when jobs are filtered by the TIMEOUT state, even though the actual memory efficiency should be the same regardless of the filter.

1.4 Steps to reproduce:
1. Run `reportseff -u $U` (shows correct MemEff values)
2. Run `reportseff -u $U -s TIMEOUT` (shows MemEff as 0.0% for TIMEOUT jobs)

1.5 Best practices for testing and fixing:
- Need to understand how MemEff is calculated normally
- Need to understand how the -s flag affects the calculation
- Should create test cases that reproduce both scenarios
- Fix should be minimal and not affect other functionality
- Need to ensure existing tests still pass</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 2. RUNNING: Setting up and Running Tests

     5	
     6	# `reportseff`
     7	
     8	> A python script for tabular display of slurm efficiency information
     9	
    10	![Example](https://github.com/troycomi/reportseff/raw/main/imgs/example.png)
    11	
    12	## About
    13	
    14	### Motivation
    15	
    16	Whether a sys admin or cluster user, knowing how well you are estimating job
    17	resources can help streamline job scheduling and maximize your priority. If you
    18	have ever tried to use `sacct` you probably had some trouble interpreting the
    19	output.  While `seff` or `jobstats` can provide detailed summaries, they don't
    20	scale easily to array jobs or offer a way to see all the jobs from a single
    21	user.  `reportseff` aims to fill this role.  Read more about the [motivation
    22	for reportseff](https://github.com/troycomi/reportseff/blob/main/ABOUT.md).
    23	
    24	### Audience
    25	
    26	If you are running more than one slurm job at a time, you should try
    27	`reportseff`.  Users of HPC systems can get an idea how well they estimate
    28	resource usage.  By tuning these values, you can get scheduled earlier and not
    29	be penalized for unused allocations.  Since `reportseff` can parse job ids from
    30	slurm output files, it simplifies the task of identifying which jobs have
    31	failed and why. Sys admins can pipe `reportseff` output to identify users with
    32	poor utilization or produce summaries at the end of a billing cycle.
    33	
    34	### Implementation
    35	
    36	`reportseff` is a wrapper around `sacct` that provides more complex option
    37	parsing, simpler options, and cleaner, colored outputs.  All querying is
    38	performed in a single call to `sacct` and should have similar performance.
    39	Multi-node and GPU utilization is acquired from information contained in the
    40	`AdminComment` field, as generated by `jobstats`.
    41	
    42	## Usage
    43	
    44	### Installation
    45	
    46	`reportseff` runs on python >= 3.6.
    47	The only external dependency is click (>= 6.7).
    48	Calling
    49	
    50	```sh
    51	pip install --user reportseff
    52	# OR
    53	pipx install reportseff
    54	```
    55	
    56	will create command line bindings and install click.
    57	
    58	### Sample Usage
    59	
    60	Try `reportseff -u $USER` or just `reportseff` in a directory with some slurm
    61	outputs.  You may be surprised by your results!
    62	
    63	#### Single job
    64	
    65	Calling `reportseff` with a single jobid will provide equivalent information to
    66	seff for that job. `reportseff 24371789` and `reportseff map_fastq_24371789`
    67	produce the following output:
    68	
    69	```txt
    70	   JobID      State          Elapsed   CPUEff   MemEff
    71	24371789    COMPLETED       03:08:03   71.2%    45.7%
    72	```
    73	
    74	#### Single array job
    75	
    76	Providing either the raw job id or the array job id will get efficiency
    77	information for a single element of the array job. `reportseff 24220929_421`
    78	and `reportseff 24221219` generate:
    79	
    80	```txt
    81	       JobID      State          Elapsed    CPUEff   MemEff
    82	24220929_421    COMPLETED       00:09:34    99.0%    34.6%
    83	```
    84	
    85	#### Array job group
    86	
    87	If the base job id of an array is provided, all elements of the array will
    88	be added to the output. `reportseff 24220929`
    89	
    90	```txt
    91	          JobID      State          Elapsed    CPUEff   MemEff
    92	     24220929_1    COMPLETED       00:10:43    99.2%    33.4%
    93	    24220929_11    COMPLETED       00:10:10    99.2%    37.5%
    94	    24220929_21    COMPLETED       00:09:25    98.8%    36.1%
    95	    24220929_31    COMPLETED       00:09:19    98.9%    33.3%
    96	    24220929_41    COMPLETED       00:09:23    98.9%    33.3%
    97	    24220929_51    COMPLETED       00:08:02    98.5%    36.3%
    98	    ...
    99	   24220929_951    COMPLETED       00:25:12    99.5%    33.5%
   100	   24220929_961    COMPLETED       00:39:26    99.7%    34.1%
   101	   24220929_971    COMPLETED       00:24:11    99.5%    34.2%
   102	   24220929_981    COMPLETED       00:24:50    99.5%    44.3%
   103	   24220929_991    COMPLETED       00:13:05    98.7%    33.7%
   104	```
   105	
   106	#### Glob expansion of slurm outputs
   107	
   108	Because slurm output files can act as job id inputs, the following can
   109	get all seff information for a given job name:
   110	
   111	```txt
   112	slurm_out  ❯❯❯ reportseff split_ubam_24*
   113	              JobID      State          Elapsed   CPUEff   MemEff
   114	split_ubam_24342816    COMPLETED       23:30:32   99.9%    4.5%
   115	split_ubam_24342914    COMPLETED       22:40:51   99.9%    4.6%
   116	split_ubam_24393599    COMPLETED       23:43:36   99.4%    4.4%
   117	split_ubam_24393655    COMPLETED       21:36:58   99.3%    4.5%
   118	split_ubam_24418960     RUNNING        02:53:11    ---      ---
   119	split_ubam_24419972     RUNNING        01:26:26    ---      ---
   120	```
   121	
   122	#### No arguments
   123	
   124	Without arguments, reportseff will try to find slurm output files in the
   125	current directory. Combine with `watch` to monitor job progress:
   126	`watch -cn 300 reportseff --color --modified-sort`
   127	
   128	```txt
   129	                JobID           State          Elapsed   CPUEff   MemEff
   130	   split_ubam_24418960          RUNNING        02:56:14    ---      ---
   131	fastq_to_ubam_24419971          RUNNING        01:29:29    ---      ---
   132	   split_ubam_24419972          RUNNING        01:29:29    ---      ---
   133	fastq_to_ubam_24393600         COMPLETED     1-02:00:47   58.3%    41.1%
   134	    map_fastq_24419330          RUNNING        02:14:53    ---      ---
   135	    map_fastq_24419323          RUNNING        02:15:24    ---      ---
   136	    map_fastq_24419324          RUNNING        02:15:24    ---      ---
   137	    map_fastq_24419322          RUNNING        02:15:24    ---      ---
   138	mark_adapters_24418437         COMPLETED       01:29:23   99.8%    48.2%
   139	mark_adapters_24418436         COMPLETED       01:29:03   99.9%    47.4%
   140	```
   141	
   142	#### Filtering slurm output files
   143	
   144	One useful application of `reportseff` is filtering a directory of slurm output
   145	files based on the state or time since running. Additionally, if only the
   146	`jobid` is specified as a format output, the filenames will be returned in a
   147	pipe-friendly manner:
   148	
   149	```txt
   150	old_runs   ❯❯❯ reportseff --since d=4 --state Timeout
   151	
   152	                 JobID   State      Elapsed  CPUEff   MemEff
   153	call_variants_31550458  TIMEOUT    20:05:17  99.5%     0.0%
   154	call_variants_31550474  TIMEOUT    20:05:17  99.6%     0.0%
   155	call_variants_31550500  TIMEOUT    20:05:08  99.7%     0.0%
   156	old_runs   ❯❯❯ reportseff --since d=4 --state Timeout --format jobid
   157	call_variants_31550458
   158	call_variants_31550474
   159	call_variants_31550500
   160	```
   161	
   162	To find all lines with `output:` in jobs which have timed out or failed
   163	in the last 4 days:
   164	
   165	```sh
   166	reportseff --since 'd=4' --state TO,F --format jobid | xargs grep output:
   167	```
   168	
   169	### Arguments
   170	
   171	Jobs can be passed as arguments in the following ways:
   172	
   173	- Job ID such as 1234567.  If the id is part of an array job, only the element
   174	for that ID will be displayed.  If the id is the base part of an array job,
   175	all elements in the array will be displayed.
   176	- Array Job ID such as 1234567\_89.  Will display only the element specified.
   177	- Slurm output file.  Format must be BASE\_%A\_%a.  BASE is optional as is a
   178	'.out' suffix.  Unix glob expansions can also be used to filter which jobs
   179	are displayed.
   180	- From current directory.  If no argument is supplied, `reportseff` will attempt
   181	to find slurm output files in the current directory as described above.
   182	If a user is provided, instead `reportseff` will show recent jobs for that user.
   183	If only `since` is set, all recent jobs for all users will be shown (if allowed).
   184	- Supplying a directory as a single argument will override the current
   185	directory to check for slurm outputs.
   186	
   187	### Options
   188	
   189	- `--color/--no-color`: Force color output or not.  By default, will force color
   190	  output.  With the no-color flag, click will strip color codes for everything
   191	  besides stdout.
   192	- `--modified-sort`: Instead of sorting by filename/jobid, sort by last
   193	  modification time of the slurm output file.
   194	- `--debug`: Write sacct result to stderr.
   195	- `--user/-u`: Ignore job arguments and instead query sacct with provided user.
   196	  Returns all jobs from the last week.
   197	- `--state/-s`: Output only jobs with states matching one of the provided options.
   198	  Accepts comma separated values of job codes (e.g. 'R') or full names
   199	  (e.g. RUNNING).  Case insensitive.
   200	- `--not-state/-S`: Output only jobs with states not matching any of the provided options.
   201	  Accepts comma separated values of job codes (e.g. 'R') or full names
   202	  (e.g. RUNNING).  Case insensitive.
   203	- `--format`: Provide a comma separated list of columns to produce. Prefixing the
   204	  argument with `+` adds the specified values to the defaults.  Values can
   205	  be any valid column name to sacct and the custom efficiency values: TimeEff,
   206	  cpuEff, MemEff.  Can also optionally set alignment (<, ^, >) and maximum width.
   207	  Default is center-aligned with a width of the maximum column entry.  For
   208	  example, `--format 'jobid%>,state%10,memeff%<5'` produces 3 columns with:
   209	  - JobId aligned right, width set automatically
   210	  - State with width 10 (center aligned by default)
   211	  - MemEff aligned left, width 5
   212	- `--slurm-format`: The filename pattern passed to sbatch during job submission.
   213	  Overrides the default regex for job id parsing from filenames.  E.g. to match
   214	  filenames like `123456.out` set `--slurm-format %j.out`.
   215	- `--since`: Limit results to those occurring after the specified time.  Accepts
   216	  sacct formats and a comma separated list of key/value pairs.  To get jobs in
   217	  the last hour and a half, can pass `h=1,m=30`.
   218	-`--until`: Limit results to those occurring before the specified time. Accepts
   219	  sacct formats and a comma separated list of key/value pairs.
   220	  Useful in combination with the 'since' option to query a specific range.
   221	- `--partition`: Limit results to a specific partition.
   222	- `--node/-n`: Display information for multi-node jobs; requires additional
   223	  sacct fields from jobstats.
   224	- `--node-and-gpu/-g`: Display information for multi-node jobs and GPU information;
   225	  requires additional sacct fields from jobstats.
   226	- `--parsable/-p`: Ignore formatting and output as a `|` delimited table.  Useful
   227	  for piping into more complex analyses.
   228	
   229	## Status, Contributions, and Support
   230	
   231	`reportseff` is actively maintained but currently feature complete.  If there
   232	is a function missing, please open an issue to discuss its merit!
   233	
   234	Bug reports, pull requests, and any feedback are welcome! Prior to submitting
   235	a pull request, be sure any new features have been tested and all unit tests
   236	are passing. In the cloned repo with
   237	[poetry](https://github.com/python-poetry/poetry#installation) installed:
   238	
   239	```sh
   240	poetry install
   241	poetry run pytest
   242	poetry run pre-commit install
   243	nox
   244	```
   245	
   246	## Troubleshooting
   247	
   248	### I can't install, what is pip?
   249	
   250	[pip](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/) is the package installer for python.  If
   251	you get an error that pip isn't found, look for a python/anaconda/conda module.
   252	[pipx](https://pypa.github.io/pipx/) ensures that each application is installed
   253	in an isolated environment.  This resolves issues of dependency versions and
   254	allows applications to be run from any environment.
   255	
   256	### The output has no color with many jobs!
   257	
   258	Click should determine if the output supports color display and react automatically
   259	in a way you expect.  Check that your terminal is setup to display colors and
   260	that your pager (probably less) will display color by default.  Some commands,
   261	e.g. `watch` aren't handled properly even when invoked to support color.  Here
   262	are some useful settings for your `.bashrc`:
   263	```
   264	# have less display colors by default.  Will fix `reportseff` not showing colors
   265	export LESS="-R"
   266	# for watch aliases, include the `--color` option
   267	watch -cn 300 reportseff --color --modified-sort
   268	#      ^                 ^^^^^^^
   269	```
   270	You can always for display of color (or suppress it) with the `--color/--no-color`
   271	options
   272	
   273	### I get an error about broken pipes when chaining to other commands
   274	
   275	Python will report that the consumer of process output has closed the stream
   276	(i.e. the pipe) while still attempting to write.  Newer versions of click
   277	should suppress the warning output, but it seems to not always work.  Besides
   278	some extra printing on stderr, the output is not affected.
   279

    98	    def update(self, entry: dict) -> None:
    99	        """Update the job properties based on the db_inquirer entry.
   100	
   101	        Args:
   102	            entry: the db_inquirer entry for the matching job
   103	        """
   104	        if "." not in entry["JobID"]:
   105	            self.state = entry["State"].split()[0]
   106	
   107	        if self.state == "PENDING":
   108	            self._cache_entries()
   109	            return
   110	
   111	        # main job id
   112	        if self.jobid == entry["JobID"]:
   113	            self._update_main_job(entry)
   114	            self._cache_entries()
   115	
   116	        elif self.state != "RUNNING":
   117	            for k, value in entry.items():
   118	                if k not in self.other_entries or not self.other_entries[k]:
   119	                    self.other_entries[k] = value
   120	            mem = parsemem(entry["MaxRSS"]) if "MaxRSS" in entry else 0
   121	            tasks = int(entry.get("NTasks", 1))
   122	            self.stepmem = max(self.stepmem, mem * tasks)
   123	
   124	            if "TRESUsageOutAve" in entry:
   125	                self.energy = max(
   126	                    self.energy,
   127	                    _parse_energy(entry["TRESUsageOutAve"]),
   128	                )
   129	
   130	    def _update_main_job(self, entry: dict) -> None:
   131	        """Update properties for the main job.
   132	
   133	        Args:
   134	            entry: the entry where the jobid matches exactly, e.g. not batch or ex
   135	        """
   136	        for k, value in entry.items():
   137	            if k not in self.other_entries or not self.other_entries[k]:
   138	                self.other_entries[k] = value
   139	        self.time = entry.get("Elapsed")
   140	
   141	        requested = 0
   142	        if "Timelimit" in entry and entry["Timelimit"] not in (
   143	            "UNLIMITED",
   144	            "Partition_Limit",
   145	        ):
   146	            requested = _parse_slurm_timedelta(entry["Timelimit"])
   147	
   148	        wall = _parse_slurm_timedelta(entry["Elapsed"]) if "Elapsed" in entry else 0
   149	
   150	        if requested != 0:
   151	            self.time_eff = round(wall / requested * 100, 1)
   152	
   153	        if self.state == "RUNNING":
   154	            return
   155	
   156	        total_cpu = _parse_slurm_timedelta(entry.get("TotalCPU", "00:00.000"))
   157	        alloc_cpus = int(entry.get("AllocCPUS", 0))
   158	
   159	        cpu_time = total_cpu / alloc_cpus if alloc_cpus != 0 else 0.0
   160	
   161	        if wall == 0:
   162	            self.cpu = None
   163	        else:
   164	            self.cpu = round(cpu_time / wall * 100, 1)
   165	
   166	        if "REQMEM" in entry and "NNodes" in entry and "AllocCPUS" in entry:
   167	            self.totalmem = parsemem(
   168	                entry["REQMEM"], int(entry["NNodes"]), int(entry["AllocCPUS"])
   169	            )
   170	
   171	        if (
   172	            "AdminComment" in entry
   173	            and len(entry["AdminComment"]) > ADMIN_COMMENT_MIN_LENGTH
   174	        ):
   175	            self._parse_admin_comment(entry["AdminComment"])
   176	
   177	    def _parse_admin_comment(self, comment: str) -> None:
   178	        """Use admin command to override efficiency values.
   179	
   180	        Decodes and parses admincommand from jobstats.
   181	
   182	        Args:
   183	            comment: The AdminComment field.
   184	        """
   185	        data = _parse_admin_comment_to_dict(comment)
   186	
   187	        if data is None:
   188	            return
   189	
   190	        for node, node_data in data["nodes"].items():
   191	            self.comment_data[node] = _get_node_data(data, node_data)
   192	
   193	        self.cpu = _average_nested_dict("CPUEff", self.comment_data)
   194	        self.mem_eff = _average_nested_dict("MemEff", self.comment_data)
   195	        if data["gpus"]:
   196	            self.gpu = _average_nested_dict("GPUEff", self.comment_data)
   197	            self.gpu_mem = _average_nested_dict("GPUMem", self.comment_data)
   198	
   199	    def _cache_entries(self) -> None:
   200	        self.other_entries["State"] = self.state
   201	        self.other_entries["TimeEff"] = self.time_eff
   202	        self.other_entries["CPUEff"] = self.cpu if self.cpu else "---"
   203	        self.other_entries["GPUEff"] = self.gpu if self.gpu is not None else "---"
   204	        if self.gpu_mem is not None:
   205	            self.other_entries["GPUMem"] = self.gpu_mem
   206	        else:
   207	            self.other_entries["GPUMem"] = "---"
   208	
   209	    def name(self) -> str:
   210	        """The name of the job.
   211	
   212	        Returns:
   213	            The filename (if set) or jobid
   214	        """
   215	        if self.filename:
   216	            return self.filename
   217	        return self.jobid
   218	
   219	    def get_entry(self, key: str) -> Any:
   220	        """Get an attribute by name.
   221	
   222	        Args:
   223	            key: the attribute to query
   224	
   225	        Returns:
   226	            The value of that attribute or "---" if not found
   227	        """
   228	        if key == "MemEff":
   229	            if self.mem_eff:  # set by admin comment
   230	                return self.mem_eff
   231	            if self.totalmem:
   232	                return round(self.stepmem / self.totalmem * 100, 1)
   233	            return "---"
   234	
   235	        if key == "Energy":
   236	            return self.energy
   237	
   238	        return self.other_entries.get(key, "---")
   239	
   240	    def get_node_entries(
   241	        self, key: str, *, gpu: bool = False
   242	    ) -> Generator[Any, None, None]:
   243	        """Get an attribute by name for each node in job.
   244	
   245	        Args:
   246	            key: the attribute to query
   247	            gpu: True if gpu values should also be split
   248	
   249	        Yields:
   250	            A generator with values for provided attribute.  If the key does
   251	            not change in nodes/gpus it will yield an empty string
   252	        """
   253	        yield self.get_entry(key)
   254	        if len(self.comment_data) > 1 or (gpu and self.gpu is not None):
   255	            for node, data in self.comment_data.items():
   256	                # get node-level data
   257	                if key == "JobID":
   258	                    yield f"  {node}"
   259	                else:
   260	                    to_yield = data.get(key, "")
   261	                    to_yield = (
   262	                        to_yield if isinstance(to_yield, str) else round(to_yield, 1)
   263	                    )
   264	                    yield to_yield
   265	                if (
   266	                    gpu and self.gpu is not None and "gpus" in data
   267	                ):  # has gpus to report
   268	                    for gpu_name, gpu_data in data["gpus"].items():
   269	                        if key == "JobID":
   270	                            yield f"    {gpu_name}"
   271	                        else:
   272	                            to_yield = gpu_data.get(key, "")
   273	                            to_yield = (
   274	                                to_yield
   275	                                if isinstance(to_yield, str)
   276	                                else round(to_yield, 1)
   277	                            )
   278	                            yield to_yield

   280	
   281	def _parse_slurm_timedelta(delta: str) -> int:
   282	    """Parse one of the three formats used in TotalCPU.
   283	
   284	    Based on which regex matches, convert into a timedelta
   285	    and return total seconds.
   286	
   287	    Args:
   288	        delta: The time duration
   289	
   290	    Returns:
   291	        Number of seconds elapsed during the delta
   292	
   293	    Raises:
   294	        ValueError: if unable to parse delta
   295	    """
   296	    match = re.match(DDHHMMSS_RE, delta)
   297	    if match:
   298	        return int(
   299	            timedelta(
   300	                days=int(match.group("days")),
   301	                hours=int(match.group("hours")),
   302	                minutes=int(match.group("minutes")),
   303	                seconds=int(match.group("seconds")),
   304	            ).total_seconds()
   305	        )
   306	    match = re.match(HHMMSS_RE, delta)
   307	    if match:
   308	        return int(
   309	            timedelta(
   310	                hours=int(match.group("hours")),
   311	                minutes=int(match.group("minutes")),
   312	                seconds=int(match.group("seconds")),
   313	            ).total_seconds()
   314	        )
   315	    match = re.match(MMSSMMM_RE, delta)
   316	    if match:
   317	        return int(
   318	            timedelta(
   319	                minutes=int(match.group("minutes")),
   320	                seconds=int(match.group("seconds")),
   321	                milliseconds=int(match.group("milliseconds")),
   322	            ).total_seconds()
   323	        )
   324	    msg = f"Failed to parse time {delta!r}"
   325	    raise ValueError(msg)
   326	
   327	
   328	def parsemem(mem: str, nodes: int = 1, cpus: int = 1) -> float:
   329	    """Parse memory representations of reqmem and maxrss.
   330	
   331	    Args:
   332	        mem: the memory representation
   333	        nodes: the number of nodes in the job
   334	        cpus: the number of cpus in the job
   335	
   336	    Returns:
   337	        The number of bytes for the job.
   338	        if mem is empty, return 0.
   339	        if mem ends with n or c, scale by the provided nodes or cpus respectively
   340	        the multiple of memory (e.g. M or G) is always scaled if provided
   341	
   342	    Raises:
   343	        ValueError: if unable to parse mem
   344	    """
   345	    if mem in ("", "0"):
   346	        return 0
   347	    match = re.fullmatch(MEM_RE, mem)
   348	    if not match:
   349	        msg = f"Failed to parse memory {mem!r}"
   350	        raise ValueError(msg)
   351	    memory = float(match.group("memory"))
   352	
   353	    if match.group("multiple") != "":
   354	        memory *= multiple_map[match.group("multiple")]
   355	
   356	    if match.group("type") != "":
   357	        if match.group("type") == "n":
   358	            memory *= nodes
   359	        else:
   360	            memory *= cpus
   361	    return memory
   362	
   363	
   364	def _parse_energy(tres: str) -> int:
   365	    """Parse energy usage from tres entry.
   366	
   367	    Args:
   368	        tres: the tres entry from sacct
   369	
   370	    Returns:
   371	        The energy usage for the job.  If missing, will return 0.
   372	    """
   373	    for entry in tres.split(","):
   374	        tokens = entry.split("=")
   375	        if tokens[0] == "energy":
   376	            return int(tokens[1])
   377	    return 0
   378	
   379	
   380	def _parse_admin_comment_to_dict(comment: str) -> dict | None:
   381	    """Attempt to parse AdminComment.
   382	
   383	    Args:
   384	        comment: The AdminComment field.
   385	
   386	    Returns:
   387	        the decoded dict
   388	        None if the comment isn't recognized but can be ignored
   389	
   390	    Raises:
   391	        ValueError: if the comment doesn't start with JS1.
   392	        ValueError: if the comment can't be decoded.
   393	    """
   394	    comment_type = comment[:3]
   395	
   396	    if not comment_type.startswith("JS"):
   397	        # ignore comments that aren't from jobstats (JS)
   398	        return None
   399	
   400	    if comment_type not in ("JS1",):
   401	        msg = f"Unknown comment type {comment_type!r}"
   402	        raise ValueError(msg)
   403	    try:
   404	        return json.loads(gzip.decompress(base64.b64decode(comment[4:])))
   405	    except Exception as exception:
   406	        msg = f"Cannot decode comment {comment!r}"
   407	        raise ValueError(msg) from exception
   408	
   409	
   410	def _get_node_data(comment_data: dict, node_data: dict) -> dict:
   411	    """Parse node level data from admin comment values.
   412	
   413	    Args:
   414	        comment_data: The AdminComment field.
   415	        node_data: Data for this node.
   416	
   417	    Returns:
   418	        the dict with efficiency information for this node
   419	    """
   420	
   421	    def get_gpu_value(comment_data: dict, key: str, gpu_number: int) -> float:
   422	        if key in comment_data and gpu_number in comment_data[key]:
   423	            return comment_data[key][gpu_number]
   424	        return 0
   425	
   426	    result = {
   427	        "MemEff": node_data["used_memory"] / node_data["total_memory"] * 100,
   428	    }
   429	
   430	    if node_data["cpus"] == 0 or comment_data["total_time"] == 0:
   431	        result["CPUEff"] = 0
   432	    else:
   433	        time_per_cpu = node_data["total_time"] / node_data["cpus"]
   434	        result["CPUEff"] = time_per_cpu / comment_data["total_time"] * 100
   435	
   436	    if comment_data["gpus"] and "gpu_total_memory" in node_data:
   437	        result["gpus"] = {
   438	            gpu: {
   439	                "GPUEff": get_gpu_value(node_data, "gpu_utilization", gpu),
   440	                "GPUMem": round(
   441	                    get_gpu_value(node_data, "gpu_used_memory", gpu)
   442	                    / get_gpu_value(node_data, "gpu_total_memory", gpu)
   443	                    * 100,
   444	                    1,
   445	                ),
   446	            }
   447	            for gpu in node_data["gpu_total_memory"]
   448	        }
   449	        result["GPUEff"] = _average_nested_dict("GPUEff", result["gpus"])
   450	        result["GPUMem"] = _average_nested_dict("GPUMem", result["gpus"])
   451	    return result
   452	
   453	
   454	def _average_nested_dict(nested_key: str, data: dict) -> float:
   455	    """Average nested values in data dictionary.
   456	
   457	    Args:
   458	        nested_key: The key to key for averaging
   459	        data: The dict to average.
   460	
   461	    Returns:
   462	        the mean value, rounded to one decimal
   463	    """
   464	    return round(
   465	        sum(value[nested_key] for value in data.values() if nested_key in value)
   466	        / len(data),
   467	        1,
   468	    )
   469

     2	
     3	from __future__ import annotations
     4	
     5	import datetime
     6	import re

     8	import subprocess
     9	from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
    10	from typing import Callable

    12	import click

    14	
    15	class BaseInquirer(ABC):
    16	    """Abstract interface for inquiring different schedulers."""
    17	
    18	    @abstractmethod
    19	    def __init__(self) -> None:
    20	        """Initialize a new inquirer."""
    21	
    22	    @abstractmethod
    23	    def get_valid_formats(self) -> list[str]:
    24	        """Get the valid formatting options supported by the inquirer.
    25	
    26	        Returns:
    27	            List of valid format options
    28	        """
    29	
    30	    @abstractmethod
    31	    def set_sacct_args(self, jobs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    32	        """Set arguments of sacct query.

    57	
    58	        Returns:
    59	            List of rows, where each row is a dictionary
    60	            with the columns as keys and entries as values
    61	            Output order is not garunteed to match the jobs list

    63	        """
    64	
    65	    @abstractmethod

   154	
   155	class SacctInquirer(BaseInquirer):
   156	    """Implementation of BaseInquirer for the sacct slurm function."""
   157	
   158	    def __init__(self) -> None:
   159	        """Initialize a new inquirer."""
   160	        self.default_args = "sacct -P -n --delimiter=^|^".split()
   161	        self.user: str | None = None
   162	        self.state: set | None = None
   163	        self.not_state: set | None = None
   164	        self.since: str | None = None
   165	        self.until: str | None = None
   166	        self.query_all_users: bool = False
   167	        self.partition: str | None = None
   168	        self.extra_args: str | None = None
   169	
   170	    def get_valid_formats(self) -> list[str]:
   171	        """Get the valid formatting options supported by the inquirer.
   172	
   173	        Returns:
   174	            List of valid format options
   175	
   176	        Raises:
   177	            RuntimeError: if sacct raises an error
   178	        """
   179	        command_args = "sacct --helpformat".split()
   180	        cmd_result = subprocess.run(
   181	            args=command_args,
   182	            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
   183	            encoding="utf8",
   184	            check=True,
   185	            text=True,
   186	            shell=False,
   187	        )
   188	        if cmd_result.returncode != 0:
   189	            msg = "Error retrieving sacct options with --helpformat"
   190	            raise RuntimeError(msg)
   191	        return cmd_result.stdout.split()
   192	
   193	    def set_sacct_args(self, jobs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   194	        """Set arguments of sacct query.
   195	
   196	        Args:
   197	            jobs: list of job names
   198	
   199	        Returns:
   200	            String of sacct arguments
   201	
   202	        """
   203	        args = []
   204	        if self.user:
   205	            if not self.since:
   206	                start_date = datetime.date.today() - datetime.timedelta(days=7)
   207	                self.since = start_date.strftime("%m%d%y")  # MMDDYY
   208	            args += [f"--user={self.user}"]
   209	        elif self.query_all_users:
   210	            args += ["--allusers"]
   211	        else:
   212	            args += ["--jobs=" + ",".join(jobs)]
   213	
   214	        if self.since:
   215	            args += [f"--starttime={self.since}"]
   216	        if self.partition:
   217	            args += [f"--partition={self.partition}"]
   218	        if self.until:
   219	            args += [f"--endtime={self.until}"]
   220	        if self.extra_args:
   221	            args += shlex.split(self.extra_args)
   222	        return args
   223	
   224	    def get_db_output(
   225	        self,
   226	        columns: list[str],
   227	        jobs: list[str],
   228	        debug_cmd: Callable | None = None,
   229	    ) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
   230	        """Query the database with the supplied columns.
   231	
   232	        Args:
   233	            columns: validated format names as strings
   234	            jobs: list of job names
   235	            debug_cmd: If specified, the raw output will passed to this function
   236	
   237	        Returns:
   238	            List of rows, where each row is a dictionary
   239	            with the columns as keys and entries as values
   240	            Output order is not guaranteed to match the jobs list
   241	
   242	        Raises:
   243	            RuntimeError: if sacct doesn't return properly
   244	        """
   245	        args = [*self.default_args, "--format=" + ",".join(columns)]
   246	        args += self.set_sacct_args(jobs)
   247	        try:
   248	            cmd_result = subprocess.run(
   249	                args=args,
   250	                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
   251	                encoding="utf8",
   252	                check=True,
   253	                text=True,
   254	                shell=False,
   255	            )
   256	            cmd_result.check_returncode()
   257	
   258	        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as error:
   259	            msg = f"Error running sacct!\n{error.stderr}"
   260	            raise RuntimeError(msg) from error
   261	
   262	        lines = cmd_result.stdout.split("\n")
   263	        if debug_cmd is not None:
   264	            debug_cmd("\n".join(lines))
   265	
   266	        sacct_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^")
   267	        result = [dict(zip(columns, sacct_split.split(line))) for line in lines if line]
   268	
   269	        if self.state:
   270	            # split to get first word in entries like "CANCELLED BY X"
   271	            result = [r for r in result if r["State"].split()[0] in self.state]
   272	
   273	        if self.not_state:
   274	            # split to get first word in entries like "CANCELLED BY X"
   275	            result = [r for r in result if r["State"].split()[0] not in self.not_state]
   276	
   277	        return result
   278	
   279	    def set_user(self, user: str) -> None:
   280	        """Set the collection of jobs based on the provided user.
   281	
   282	        Args:
   283	            user: user name
   284	        """
   285	        self.user = user
   286	
   287	    def set_partition(self, partition: str) -> None:
   288	        """Set the collection of jobs based on the provided partition.
   289	
   290	        Args:
   291	            partition: partition name
   292	        """
   293	        self.partition = partition
   294	
   295	    def set_extra_args(self, extra_args: str) -> None:
   296	        """Set extra arguments to be forwarded to sacct.
   297	
   298	        Args:
   299	            extra_args: list of arguments
   300	        """
   301	        self.extra_args = extra_args
   302	
   303	    def all_users(self) -> None:
   304	        """Query for all users if `since` is set."""
   305	        self.query_all_users = True
   306	
   307	    def set_state(self, state: str) -> None:
   308	        """Set the state to include output jobs.
   309	
   310	        Args:
   311	            state: comma separated list of state names or codes
   312	        """
   313	        if not state:
   314	            return
   315	
   316	        self.state = get_states_as_set(state)
   317	        # add a single value if it's empty here
   318	        if not self.state:
   319	            click.secho("No valid states provided to include", fg="yellow", err=True)
   320	            self.state.add(None)
   321	
   322	    def set_not_state(self, state: str) -> None:
   323	        """Set the state to exclude from output jobs.
   324	
   325	        Args:
   326	            state: comma separated list of state names or codes
   327	
   328	        """
   329	        if not state:
   330	            return
   331	
   332	        self.not_state = get_states_as_set(state)
   333	        # add a single value if it's empty here
   334	        if not self.not_state:
   335	            click.secho("No valid states provided to exclude", fg="yellow", err=True)
   336	            self.not_state = None
   337	
   338	    def parse_date(self, d: str) -> str:
   339	        """Parse and convert custom string date format.
   340	
   341	        Args:
   342	            d: the string of date.
   343	
   344	        Returns:
   345	            converted string of date
   346	        """
   347	        abbrev_to_key = {
   348	            "w": "weeks",
   349	            "W": "weeks",
   350	            "d": "days",
   351	            "D": "days",
   352	            "h": "hours",
   353	            "H": "hours",
   354	            "m": "minutes",
   355	            "M": "minutes",
   356	        }
   357	        valid_args = ["weeks", "days", "hours", "minutes"]
   358	        date_args = {}
   359	
   360	        args = d.split(",")
   361	        for arg in args:
   362	            if "=" not in arg:
   363	                continue
   364	
   365	            toks = arg.split("=")
   366	
   367	            # convert key to name
   368	            if toks[0] in abbrev_to_key:
   369	                toks[0] = abbrev_to_key[toks[0]]
   370	
   371	            toks[0] = toks[0].lower()
   372	
   373	            if toks[0] in valid_args:
   374	                try:
   375	                    date_args[toks[0]] = int(toks[1])
   376	                except ValueError:
   377	                    continue
   378	
   379	        date = datetime.datetime.today()
   380	        date -= datetime.timedelta(**date_args)
   381	        return date.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M")  # MMDDYY
   382	
   383	    def set_until(self, until: str) -> None:
   384	        """Set the filter for time of jobs to consider.
   385	
   386	        Args:
   387	            until: the string for filtering. If specified as time=amount
   388	                will subtract that amount from the current time
   389	        """
   390	        if not until:
   391	            return
   392	        if "=" in until:  # handle custom format
   393	            self.until = self.parse_date(until)
   394	        else:
   395	            self.until = until
   396	
   397	    def set_since(self, since: str) -> None:
   398	        """Set the filter for time of jobs to consider.
   399	
   400	        Args:
   401	            since: the string for filtering.  If specified as time=amount
   402	                will subtract that amount from the current time
   403	        """
   404	        if not since:
   405	            return
   406	        if "=" in since:  # handle custom format
   407	            self.since = self.parse_date(since)
   408	        else:
   409	            self.since = since
   410	
   411	    def has_since(self) -> bool:
   412	        """Check if since has been set.
   413	
   414	        Returns:
   415	            True if since has been set properly
   416	        """
   417	        return bool(self.since)
   418	
   419	    def get_partition_timelimits(self) -> dict:
   420	        """Get partition time limits.
   421	
   422	        Returns:
   423	            dict mapping partition names to maximum timelimits.
   424	
   425	        Raises:
   426	            RuntimeError: if scontrol raises an error
   427	        """
   428	        command_args = "scontrol show partition".split()
   429	        cmd_result = subprocess.run(
   430	            args=command_args,
   431	            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
   432	            encoding="utf8",
   433	            check=True,
   434	            text=True,
   435	            shell=False,
   436	        )
   437	        if cmd_result.returncode != 0:
   438	            msg = "Error retrieving information from scontrol"
   439	            raise RuntimeError(msg)
   440	
   441	        partition_name = re.compile(r"^PartitionName=(?P<name>\S+)$")
   442	        time_limit = re.compile(r"MaxTime=(?P<time>\S+)")
   443	
   444	        partition = ""
   445	        result = {}
   446	        for line in cmd_result.stdout.split():
   447	            match = re.match(partition_name, line)
   448	            if match:
   449	                partition = match.group("name")
   450	            match = re.match(time_limit, line)
   451	            if match:
   452	                result[partition] = match.group("time")
   453	
   454	        return result
   455	
   456	
   457	def get_states_as_set(state_list: str) -> set:
   458	    """Helper method to parse the state string.
   459	
   460	    Args:
   461	        state_list: comma separated string with codes and states
   462	
   463	    Returns:
   464	        Set with valid state names in upper case
   465	    """
   466	    codes_to_states = {
   467	        "BF": "BOOT_FAIL",
   468	        "CA": "CANCELLED",
   469	        "CD": "COMPLETED",
   470	        "DL": "DEADLINE",
   471	        "F": "FAILED",
   472	        "NF": "NODE_FAIL",
   473	        "OOM": "OUT_OF_MEMORY",
   474	        "PD": "PENDING",
   475	        "PR": "PREEMPTED",
   476	        "R": "RUNNING",
   477	        "RQ": "REQUEUED",
   478	        "RS": "RESIZING",
   479	        "RV": "REVOKED",
   480	        "S": "SUSPENDED",
   481	        "TO": "TIMEOUT",
   482	    }
   483	    possible_states = set(codes_to_states.values())
   484	    states = {
   485	        codes_to_states.get(state, state) for state in state_list.upper().split(",")
   486	    }
   487	
   488	    for state in states:
   489	        if state not in possible_states:
   490	            click.secho(f"Unknown state {state}", fg="yellow", err=True)
   491	
   492	    return states.intersection(possible_states)
   493

   137	
   138	def get_jobs(args: ReportseffParameters) -> tuple[str, int]:
   139	    """Helper method to get jobs from db_inquirer.
   140	
   141	    Returns:
   142	        The string to display, tabulated and colored
   143	        The number of jobs found to use paging properly
   144	
   145	    Raises:
   146	        Exception: if there is an error processing entries
   147	    """
   148	    job_collection = JobCollection()
   149	
   150	    if args.slurm_format:
   151	        job_collection.set_custom_seff_format(args.slurm_format)
   152	
   153	    inquirer, renderer = get_implementation(
   154	        args.format_str,
   155	        node=args.node,
   156	        node_and_gpu=args.node_and_gpu,
   157	        parsable=args.parsable,
   158	    )
   159	
   160	    inquirer.set_state(args.state)
   161	    inquirer.set_not_state(args.not_state)
   162	
   163	    inquirer.set_since(args.since)

   195	        for entry in db_output:

1. In `console.py`, the `get_jobs` function calls `inquirer.set_state(args.state)` to set the state filter
2. The `SacctInquirer.get_db_output` method queries the database and then applies state filtering at lines 269-275:
   ```python
   if self.state:
       # split to get first word in entries like "CANCELLED BY X"
       result = [r for r in result if r["State"].split()[0] in self.state]
   ```

3. However, the issue is that when state filtering is applied in the database query, it filters the sacct results BEFORE they reach the Job.update() method.

Looking at the Job class in job.py:
- Line 228-233: The `get_entry` method for MemEff checks if `self.mem_eff` is set (from admin comment) or calculates it from `self.totalmem` and `self.stepmem`
- Lines 166-169: `self.totalmem` is set from REQMEM, NNodes, AllocCPUS 
- Lines 120-122: `self.stepmem` is updated from MaxRSS and NTasks

The problem seems to be that when state filtering is applied, some entries might be filtered out that are needed for the memory calculation.

"""Script to reproduce the MemEff = 0% issue when using -s TIMEOUT."""

import sys
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from click.testing import CliRunner
import reportseff.console as console


def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Mock the subprocess result to simulate sacct output with TIMEOUT jobs
    # that have valid memory information
    
    # Simulate sacct output for a TIMEOUT job with valid memory usage
    sacct_output_with_timeout = """JS1:H4sIAPsSCGYC/2WQwU7DMAxEfyXy2ZXtpk2df8FTVSFSo0YkSFSlcED8O05QkYB7ezye8cw0mJhOpAM5kxq3j90B1jB9Dg6YzqR72MEmJ9Kj7GBZkx7mDazXpMd5C+sN6WneQfkF5XfpNq9gvSXdyQ6WNek+tbDekR7mHaz3pMd5D+UDlI/SbV7Dek+6lx0sa9JDamG9Iz3MO1gfSI/zEdZPUL5KN3kD6wPpUXawrEkPaQvrI+lh3sH6SHqcz7B+hvJMushbWB9Jz7KDZU16Sm1efAH5xTL3ACgAAA==|1|03:00:00|24371789|24371789||1|4Gn|TIMEOUT|03:00:00|00:13:17|1
JS1:H4sIAPsSCGYC/2WQwU7DMAxEfyXy2ZXtpk2df8FTVSFSo0YkSFSlcED8O05QkYB7ezye8cw0mJhOpAM5kxq3j90B1jB9Dg6YzqR72MEmJ9Kj7GBZkx7mDazXpMd5C+sN6WneQfkF5XfpNq9gvSXdyQ6WNek+tbDekR7mHaz3pMd5D+UDlI/SbV7Dek+6lx0sa9JDamG9Iz3MO1gfSI/zEdZPUL5KN3kD6wPpUXawrEkPaQvrI+lh3sH6SHqcz7B+hvJMushbWB9Jz7KDZU16Sm1efAH5xTL3ACgAAA==|1|03:00:00|24371789.batch|24371789.batch|2G|1||TIMEOUT|03:00:00|00:13:17|1"""

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Mock subprocess.run to return our simulated sacct output
    import subprocess
    original_run = subprocess.run
    
    def mock_subprocess_run(*args, **kwargs):
        result = MagicMock()
        result.returncode = 0
        result.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout
        return result
    
    # Patch subprocess.run
    subprocess.run = mock_subprocess_run
    
    try:
        # Test 1: Without state filter (should show proper MemEff)
        print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
        result1 = runner.invoke(
            console.main,
            "--no-color 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
        )
        print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
        print("Output:")
        print(result1.output)
        
        # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter (should show MemEff = 0% - the bug)
        print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
        result2 = runner.invoke(
            console.main,
            "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
        )
        print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
        print("Output:")
        print(result2.output)
        
        # Compare the MemEff values
        lines1 = result1.output.strip().split('\n')
        lines2 = result2.output.strip().split('\n')
        
        if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
            # Find the job line (skip header)
            job_line1 = None
            job_line2 = None
            
            for line in lines1[1:]:
                if '24371789' in line:
                    job_line1 = line
                    break
                    
            for line in lines2[1:]:
                if '24371789' in line:
                    job_line2 = line
                    break
            
            if job_line1 and job_line2:
                parts1 = job_line1.split()
                parts2 = job_line2.split()
                
                if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                    mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                    mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                    
                    print(f"\nComparison:")
                    print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                    print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                    
                    if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                        print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                        return True
                    else:
                        print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                        return False
                else:
                    print("Could not parse MemEff values from output")
            else:
                print("Could not find job line in output")
        else:
            print("Insufficient output lines")
            
    finally:
        # Restore original subprocess.run
        subprocess.run = original_run
    
    return False


if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = test_timeout_mem_eff_issue()
    sys.exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/reproduce_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3 && python reproduce_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Test 1: Without state filter ===

def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Mock the subprocess result to simulate sacct output with TIMEOUT jobs
    # that have valid memory information
    
    # Simulate sacct output for a TIMEOUT job with valid memory usage
    sacct_output_with_timeout = """JS1:H4sIAPsSCGYC/2WQwU7DMAxEfyXy2ZXtpk2df8FTVSFSo0YkSFSlcED8O05QkYB7ezye8cw0mJhOpAM5kxq3j90B1jB9Dg6YzqR72MEmJ9Kj7GBZkx7mDazXpMd5C+sN6WneQfkF5XfpNq9gvSXdyQ6WNek+tbDekR7mHaz3pMd5D+UDlI/SbV7Dek+6lx0sa9JDamG9Iz3MO1gfSI/zEdZPUL5KN3kD6wPpUXawrEkPaQvrI+lh3sH6SHqcz7B+hvJMushbWB9Jz7KDZU16Sm1efAH5xTL3ACgAAA==|1|03:00:00|24371789|24371789||1|4Gn|TIMEOUT|03:00:00|00:13:17|1
JS1:H4sIAPsSCGYC/2WQwU7DMAxEfyXy2ZXtpk2df8FTVSFSo0YkSFSlcED8O05QkYB7ezye8cw0mJhOpAM5kxq3j90B1jB9Dg6YzqR72MEmJ9Kj7GBZkx7mDazXpMd5C+sN6WneQfkF5XfpNq9gvSXdyQ6WNek+tbDekR7mHaz3pMd5D+UDlI/SbV7Dek+6lx0sa9JDamG9Iz3MO1gfSI/zEdZPUL5KN3kD6wPpUXawrEkPaQvrI+lh3sH6SHqcz7B+hvJMushbWB9Jz7KDZU16Sm1efAH5xTL3ACgAAA==|1|03:00:00|24371789.batch|24371789.batch|2G|1||TIMEOUT|03:00:00|00:13:17|1"""

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Create mock for subprocess.run
    mock_subprocess = MagicMock()
    mock_subprocess.returncode = 0
    mock_subprocess.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout
    
    # Mock which to return True for sacct
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess):
            # Test 1: Without state filter (should show proper MemEff)
            print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
            result1 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result1.output))
            print(result1.output)
            
            # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter (should show MemEff = 0% - the bug)
            print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
            result2 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result2.output))
            print(result2.output)
            
            # Compare the MemEff values
            lines1 = result1.output.strip().split('\n')
            lines2 = result2.output.strip().split('\n')
            
            if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                # Find the job line (skip header)
                job_line1 = None
                job_line2 = None
                
                for line in lines1[1:]:
                    if '24371789' in line and line.strip():
                        job_line1 = line
                        break
                        
                for line in lines2[1:]:
                    if '24371789' in line and line.strip():
                        job_line2 = line
                        break
                
                if job_line1 and job_line2:
                    parts1 = job_line1.split()
                    parts2 = job_line2.split()
                    
                    if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                        mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                        mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                        
                        print(f"\nComparison:")
                        print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                        print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                        
                        if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                            print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                            return True
                        else:
                            print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print("Could not parse MemEff values from output")
                        print(f"Parts1: {parts1}")
                        print(f"Parts2: {parts2}")
                else:
                    print("Could not find job line in output")
                    print(f"Job line 1: {job_line1}")
                    print(f"Job line 2: {job_line2}")
            else:
                print("Insufficient output lines")
                print(f"Lines1 count: {len(lines1)}")
                print(f"Lines2 count: {len(lines2)}")
                print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
                print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")

    mock_subprocess = MagicMock()
    mock_subprocess.returncode = 0
    mock_subprocess.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout
    
    # Mock which to return True for sacct
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess):
            # Test 1: Without state filter (should show proper MemEff)
            print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
            result1 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result1.output))
            print(result1.output)
            
            # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter (should show MemEff = 0% - the bug)
            print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
            result2 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 24371789 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result2.output))
            print(result2.output)
            
            # Compare the MemEff values
            lines1 = result1.output.strip().split('\n')
            lines2 = result2.output.strip().split('\n')
            
            if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                # Find the job line (skip header)
                job_line1 = None
                job_line2 = None
                
                for line in lines1[1:]:
                    if '24371789' in line and line.strip():
                        job_line1 = line
                        break
                        
                for line in lines2[1:]:
                    if '24371789' in line and line.strip():
                        job_line2 = line
                        break
                
                if job_line1 and job_line2:
                    parts1 = job_line1.split()
                    parts2 = job_line2.split()
                    
                    if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                        mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                        mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                        
                        print(f"\nComparison:")
                        print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                        print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                        
                        if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                            print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                            return True
                        else:
                            print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print("Could not parse MemEff values from output")
                        print(f"Parts1: {parts1}")
                        print(f"Parts2: {parts2}")
                else:
                    print("Could not find job line in output")
                    print(f"Job line 1: {job_line1}")
                    print(f"Job line 2: {job_line2}")
            else:
                print("Insufficient output lines")
                print(f"Lines1 count: {len(lines1)}")
                print(f"Lines2 count: {len(lines2)}")
                print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
                print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")

"""Script to reproduce the MemEff = 0% issue when using -s TIMEOUT."""

import sys
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
from click.testing import CliRunner
import reportseff.console as console


def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Create sacct output that simulates a TIMEOUT job with valid memory info
    # Using format: delimiter-separated columns matching what reportseff expects
    # Columns: AdminComment^|^AllocCPUS^|^Elapsed^|^JobID^|^JobIDRaw^|^MaxRSS^|^NNodes^|^REQMEM^|^State^|^Timelimit^|^TotalCPU^|^NTasks
    sacct_output_without_filter = (
        # Main job entry
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678^|^12345678^|^^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry with memory usage
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678.batch^|^12345678.batch^|^2G^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
    )
    
    sacct_output_with_timeout_filter = sacct_output_without_filter

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Mock which to return True for sacct
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        # Test 1: Without state filter
        print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
        mock_subprocess1 = MagicMock()
        mock_subprocess1.returncode = 0
        mock_subprocess1.stdout = sacct_output_without_filter
        
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess1):
            result1 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result1.output))
            print(result1.output)
            
            # Check if there was an error
            if result1.exit_code != 0:
                print("STDERR:", result1.output)
        
        # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter  
        print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
        mock_subprocess2 = MagicMock()
        mock_subprocess2.returncode = 0
        mock_subprocess2.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout_filter
        
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess2):
            result2 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result2.output))
            print(result2.output)
            
            # Check if there was an error
            if result2.exit_code != 0:
                print("STDERR:", result2.output)
            
            # Compare the MemEff values
            lines1 = [line for line in result1.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
            lines2 = [line for line in result2.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
            
            print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
            print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")
            
            if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                # Parse the job lines (skip headers)
                job_lines1 = [line for line in lines1[1:] if '12345678' in line]
                job_lines2 = [line for line in lines2[1:] if '12345678' in line]
                
                if job_lines1 and job_lines2:
                    parts1 = job_lines1[0].split()
                    parts2 = job_lines2[0].split()
                    
                    print(f"Parsed parts 1: {parts1}")
                    print(f"Parsed parts 2: {parts2}")
                    
                    if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                        mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                        mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                        
                        print(f"\nComparison:")
                        print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                        print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                        
                        # Check if MemEff becomes 0.0% with filter (the bug)
                        if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                            print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                            return True
                        elif mem_eff1 == mem_eff2:
                            print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                            return False
                        else:
                            print(f"Different issue: MemEff changed from {mem_eff1} to {mem_eff2}")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print("Could not parse enough columns from output")
                else:
                    print("Could not find job lines in output")
                    print(f"Job lines 1: {job_lines1}")
                    print(f"Job lines 2: {job_lines2}")
            else:
                print("Insufficient output lines")
                print(f"Non-empty lines1 count: {len([l for l in lines1 if l.strip()])}")
                print(f"Non-empty lines2 count: {len([l for l in lines2 if l.strip()])}")
                
    return False</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/reproduce_issue.py has been edited. Here's the result of running `cat -n` on a snippet of /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/reproduce_issue.py:

import base64
import gzip
import json

import pytest


def to_comment(info: dict) -> str:
    """Convert jobstats dict to compressed base64 to match AdminComment."""
    return "JS1:" + base64.b64encode(
        gzip.compress(json.dumps(info, sort_keys=True, indent=4).encode("ascii"))
    ).decode("ascii")


@pytest.fixture()
def get_jobstats():
    """Fixture to produce jobstats-like encoding."""
    return to_comment


def to_sacct_dict(sacct_line: str) -> dict:
    """Convert debug print statement to dictionary like from sacct."""
    columns = (
        "AdminComment",
        "AllocCPUS",
        "Elapsed",
        "JobID",
        "JobIDRaw",
        "MaxRSS",
        "NNodes",
        "REQMEM",
        "State",
        "Timelimit",

"""Script to reproduce the MemEff = 0% issue when using -s TIMEOUT."""

import sys
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
from click.testing import CliRunner
import reportseff.console as console


def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Create sacct output that simulates a TIMEOUT job with valid memory info
    # Using format: delimiter-separated columns matching what reportseff expects
    # Columns: AdminComment^|^AllocCPUS^|^Elapsed^|^JobID^|^JobIDRaw^|^MaxRSS^|^NNodes^|^REQMEM^|^State^|^Timelimit^|^TotalCPU^|^NTasks
    sacct_output_without_filter = (
        # Main job entry
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678^|^12345678^|^^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry with memory usage
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678.batch^|^12345678.batch^|^2G^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
    )
    
    sacct_output_with_timeout_filter = sacct_output_without_filter

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Mock which to return True for sacct
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        # Test 1: Without state filter
        print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
        mock_subprocess1 = MagicMock()
        mock_subprocess1.returncode = 0
        mock_subprocess1.stdout = sacct_output_without_filter
        
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess1):
            result1 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result1.output))
            print(result1.output)
            
            # Check if there was an error
            if result1.exit_code != 0:
                print("STDERR:", result1.output)
        
        # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter  
        print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
        mock_subprocess2 = MagicMock()
        mock_subprocess2.returncode = 0
        mock_subprocess2.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout_filter
        
        with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess2):
            result2 = runner.invoke(
                console.main,
                "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
            )
            print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
            print("Output:")
            print(repr(result2.output))
            print(result2.output)
            
            # Check if there was an error
            if result2.exit_code != 0:
                print("STDERR:", result2.output)
            
            # Compare the MemEff values
            lines1 = [line for line in result1.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
            lines2 = [line for line in result2.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
            
            print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
            print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")
            
            if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                # Parse the job lines (skip headers)
                job_lines1 = [line for line in lines1[1:] if '12345678' in line]
                job_lines2 = [line for line in lines2[1:] if '12345678' in line]
                
                if job_lines1 and job_lines2:
                    parts1 = job_lines1[0].split()
                    parts2 = job_lines2[0].split()
                    
                    print(f"Parsed parts 1: {parts1}")
                    print(f"Parsed parts 2: {parts2}")
                    
                    if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                        mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                        mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                        
                        print(f"\nComparison:")
                        print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                        print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                        
                        # Check if MemEff becomes 0.0% with filter (the bug)
                        if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                            print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                            return True
                        elif mem_eff1 == mem_eff2:
                            print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                            return False
                        else:
                            print(f"Different issue: MemEff changed from {mem_eff1} to {mem_eff2}")
                            return False
                    else:
                        print("Could not parse enough columns from output")
                else:
                    print("Could not find job lines in output")
                    print(f"Job lines 1: {job_lines1}")
                    print(f"Job lines 2: {job_lines2}")
            else:
                print("Insufficient output lines")
                print(f"Non-empty lines1 count: {len([l for l in lines1 if l.strip()])}")
                print(f"Non-empty lines2 count: {len([l for l in lines2 if l.strip()])}")
                
    return False</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Script to reproduce the MemEff = 0% issue when using -s TIMEOUT."""

import sys
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
from click.testing import CliRunner
import pytest
from reportseff.console import main
from reportseff.db_inquirer import SacctInquirer


def mock_valid_formats(_self):
    """Mock valid formats to prevent calls to shell."""
    return (
        "JobID,State,Elapsed,JobIDRaw,State,TotalCPU,AllocCPUS,"
        "REQMEM,NNodes,MaxRSS,Timelimit,AdminComment,NTasks"
    ).split(",")


def mock_partition_timelimits(_self):
    """Mock partition timelimits."""
    return {}


def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Create sacct output that simulates a TIMEOUT job with valid memory info
    sacct_output_without_filter = (
        # Main job entry
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678^|^12345678^|^^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry with memory usage
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678.batch^|^12345678.batch^|^2G^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
    )
    
    sacct_output_with_timeout_filter = sacct_output_without_filter

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Apply all necessary mocks
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_valid_formats", new=mock_valid_formats):
            with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_partition_timelimits", new=mock_partition_timelimits):
                
                # Test 1: Without state filter
                print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
                mock_subprocess1 = MagicMock()
                mock_subprocess1.returncode = 0
                mock_subprocess1.stdout = sacct_output_without_filter
                
                with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess1):
                    result1 = runner.invoke(
                        main,
                        "--no-color 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
                    )
                    print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
                    print("Output:")
                    print(repr(result1.output))
                    print(result1.output)
                    
                    if result1.exit_code != 0:
                        print("STDERR:", result1.output)
                
                # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter  
                print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
                mock_subprocess2 = MagicMock()
                mock_subprocess2.returncode = 0
                mock_subprocess2.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout_filter
                
                with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run', return_value=mock_subprocess2):
                    result2 = runner.invoke(
                        main,
                        "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
                    )
                    print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
                    print("Output:")
                    print(repr(result2.output))
                    print(result2.output)
                    
                    if result2.exit_code != 0:
                        print("STDERR:", result2.output)
                    
                    # Compare the MemEff values
                    lines1 = [line for line in result1.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
                    lines2 = [line for line in result2.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
                    
                    print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
                    print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")
                    
                    if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                        # Parse the job lines (skip headers)
                        job_lines1 = [line for line in lines1[1:] if '12345678' in line]

                            parts2 = job_lines2[0].split()

                                    print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                                    return False
                                else:

Actually, let me look at this differently. Instead of trying to recreate the exact sacct output format, let me create a much simpler reproduction script that focuses on the core issue. 

Based on the issue description, the problem is:
1. When running `reportseff -u $U` - MemEff shows correct values like 72.2%, 74.5%
2. When running `reportseff -u $U -s TIMEOUT` - MemEff shows 0.0% for TIMEOUT jobs

This suggests that the state filtering mechanism is somehow interfering with the MemEff calculation. Let me look at the actual code where state filtering happens and see if there's a connection.

Looking at db_inquirer.py lines 269-275:
```python
if self.state:
    # split to get first word in entries like "CANCELLED BY X"
    result = [r for r in result if r["State"].split()[0] in self.state]
```

And looking at job.py, the MemEff calculation depends on having both the main job entry AND the batch job entry to calculate memory efficiency properly. The main job entry has REQMEM but no MaxRSS, while the batch job entry has MaxRSS.

If state filtering happens AFTER the sacct query but BEFORE the job entries are processed, it could potentially filter out the batch job entries that are needed for memory calculation, leaving only the main job entries which don't have MaxRSS.
