def format_sacct_output(entries):
    """Format entries as sacct output."""
    lines = []
    for entry in entries:
        line_parts = [
            entry.get("AdminComment", ""),
            entry.get("AllocCPUS", ""),
            entry.get("Elapsed", ""),
            entry.get("JobID", ""),
            entry.get("JobIDRaw", ""),
            entry.get("MaxRSS", ""),
            entry.get("NNodes", ""),
            entry.get("REQMEM", ""),
            entry.get("State", ""),
            entry.get("Timelimit", ""),
            entry.get("TotalCPU", ""),
            entry.get("NTasks", ""),
        ]
        lines.append("^|^".join(line_parts))
    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"


def test_timeout_mem_eff_issue():
    """Reproduce the issue where MemEff shows 0% for TIMEOUT jobs when filtered."""
    
    # Get the test data
    test_data = get_bad_gpu_used_data()
    
    # Format as sacct output
    sacct_output_without_filter = format_sacct_output(test_data)
    sacct_output_with_timeout_filter = sacct_output_without_filter

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Apply all necessary mocks
    with patch('reportseff.console.which', return_value='/bin/sacct'):
        with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_valid_formats", new=mock_valid_formats):
            with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_partition_timelimits", new=mock_partition_timelimits):
                
                # Test 1: Without state filter
                print("=== Test 1: Without state filter ===")
                print("Sacct output:")
                print(repr(sacct_output_without_filter))
                
                with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run') as mock_subprocess:
                    mock_subprocess.return_value.returncode = 0
                    mock_subprocess.return_value.stdout = sacct_output_without_filter
                    
                    result1 = runner.invoke(
                        main,
                        "--no-color 46044267 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
                    )
                    print("Exit code:", result1.exit_code)
                    print("Output:")
                    print(repr(result1.output))
                    print(result1.output)
                    
                    if result1.exit_code != 0:
                        print("STDERR:", result1.output)
                
                # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter  
                print("\n=== Test 2: With -s TIMEOUT filter ===")
                print("Sacct output:")
                print(repr(sacct_output_with_timeout_filter))
                
                with patch('reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run') as mock_subprocess:
                    mock_subprocess.return_value.returncode = 0
                    mock_subprocess.return_value.stdout = sacct_output_with_timeout_filter
                    
                    result2 = runner.invoke(
                        main,
                        "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 46044267 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
                    )
                    print("Exit code:", result2.exit_code)
                    print("Output:")
                    print(repr(result2.output))
                    print(result2.output)
                    
                    if result2.exit_code != 0:
                        print("STDERR:", result2.output)
                    
                    # Compare the MemEff values
                    lines1 = [line for line in result1.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
                    lines2 = [line for line in result2.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
                    
                    print(f"Lines1: {lines1}")
                    print(f"Lines2: {lines2}")
                    
                    if len(lines1) > 1 and len(lines2) > 1:
                        # Parse the job lines (skip headers)
                        job_lines1 = [line for line in lines1[1:] if '46044267' in line and line.strip()]
                        job_lines2 = [line for line in lines2[1:] if '46044267' in line and line.strip()]
                        
                        if job_lines1 and job_lines2:
                            parts1 = job_lines1[0].split()
                            parts2 = job_lines2[0].split()
                            
                            print(f"Parsed parts 1: {parts1}")
                            print(f"Parsed parts 2: {parts2}")
                            
                            if len(parts1) >= 5 and len(parts2) >= 5:
                                mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff column
                                mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff column
                                
                                print(f"\nComparison:")
                                print(f"Without filter: MemEff = {mem_eff1}")
                                print(f"With TIMEOUT filter: MemEff = {mem_eff2}")
                                
                                # Check if MemEff becomes 0.0% with filter (the bug)
                                if mem_eff1 != mem_eff2 and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                                    print("BUG REPRODUCED: MemEff becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT")
                                    return True
                                elif mem_eff1 == mem_eff2:
                                    print("Bug not reproduced - MemEff values are consistent")
                                    # Check if both are 0.0% - that would indicate the issue exists but wasn't caught by comparison
                                    if mem_eff1 == "0.0%" and mem_eff2 == "0.0%":
                                        print("Both show 0.0% - this indicates the bug exists!")
                                        return True
                                    return False
                                else:
                                    print(f"Different issue: MemEff changed from {mem_eff1} to {mem_eff2}")
                                    return False
                            else:
                                print("Could not parse enough columns from output")
                        else:
                            print("Could not find job lines in output")
                            print(f"Job lines 1: {job_lines1}")
                            print(f"Job lines 2: {job_lines2}")
                    else:
                        print("Insufficient output lines")
                        print(f"Non-empty lines1 count: {len([l for l in lines1 if l.strip()])}")
                        print(f"Non-empty lines2 count: {len([l for l in lines2 if l.strip()])}")
                        
    return False


if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = test_timeout_mem_eff_issue()
    sys.exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

Let me create a proper test file:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/test_timeout_bug.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>"""Test to reproduce the MemEff = 0% issue when using -s TIMEOUT."""

import pytest
from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock
from click.testing import CliRunner
from reportseff.console import main
from reportseff.db_inquirer import SacctInquirer


def mock_valid_formats(_self):
    """Mock valid formats to prevent calls to shell."""
    return (
        "JobID,State,Elapsed,JobIDRaw,State,TotalCPU,AllocCPUS,"
        "REQMEM,NNodes,MaxRSS,Timelimit,AdminComment,NTasks"
    ).split(",")


def mock_partition_timelimits(_self):
    """Mock partition timelimits."""
    return {}


@pytest.fixture
def _mock_inquirer():
    """Override valid formats to prevent calls to shell."""

    def mock_valid(_self):
        return (
            "JobID,State,Elapsed,JobIDRaw,State,TotalCPU,AllocCPUS,"
            "REQMEM,NNodes,MaxRSS,Timelimit,AdminComment,NTasks"
        ).split(",")

    def mock_partition_timelimits(_self):
        return {}

    with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_valid_formats", new=mock_valid):
        with patch.object(
            SacctInquirer, "get_partition_timelimits", new=mock_partition_timelimits
        ):
            yield


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("_mock_inquirer")
def test_timeout_mem_eff_consistency(mocker):
    """Test that MemEff is consistent whether or not -s TIMEOUT is used."""
    mocker.patch("reportseff.console.which", return_value="/bin/sacct")
    
    # Create sacct output that simulates a TIMEOUT job with valid memory info
    # Using the same format as existing tests
    sacct_output = (
        # Main job entry
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678^|^12345678^|^^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry with memory usage
        "^|^1^|^03:00:06^|^12345678.batch^|^12345678.batch^|^2G^|^1^|^4Gn^|^TIMEOUT^|^17:00:00^|^00:13:17^|^1\n"
    )

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Test 1: Without state filter
    sub_result1 = mocker.MagicMock()
    sub_result1.returncode = 0
    sub_result1.stdout = sacct_output
    mocker.patch("reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run", return_value=sub_result1)
    
    result1 = runner.invoke(
        main,
        "--no-color 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
    )
    
    print("Result 1 output:")
    print(repr(result1.output))
    print(result1.output)
    
    assert result1.exit_code == 0
    
    # Test 2: With TIMEOUT state filter
    sub_result2 = mocker.MagicMock()
    sub_result2.returncode = 0
    sub_result2.stdout = sacct_output
    mocker.patch("reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run", return_value=sub_result2)
    
    result2 = runner.invoke(
        main,
        "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 12345678 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
    )
    
    print("Result 2 output:")
    print(repr(result2.output))
    print(result2.output)
    
    assert result2.exit_code == 0
    
    # Extract MemEff values from both results
    lines1 = [line.strip() for line in result1.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
    lines2 = [line.strip() for line in result2.output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
    
    # Find the job line in each result (skip header)
    job_line1 = None
    job_line2 = None
    
    for line in lines1[1:]:  # Skip header
        if '12345678' in line:
            job_line1 = line
            break
            
    for line in lines2[1:]:  # Skip header
        if '12345678' in line:
            job_line2 = line
            break
    
    print(f"Job line 1: {job_line1}")
    print(f"Job line 2: {job_line2}")
    
    assert job_line1 is not None, "Job not found in result 1"
    assert job_line2 is not None, "Job not found in result 2"
    
    # Parse MemEff values
    parts1 = job_line1.split()
    parts2 = job_line2.split()
    
    assert len(parts1) >= 5, f"Not enough columns in result 1: {parts1}"
    assert len(parts2) >= 5, f"Not enough columns in result 2: {parts2}"
    
    mem_eff1 = parts1[4]  # MemEff is 5th column (0-indexed 4)
    mem_eff2 = parts2[4]  # MemEff is 5th column (0-indexed 4)
    
    print(f"MemEff without filter: {mem_eff1}")
    print(f"MemEff with TIMEOUT filter: {mem_eff2}")
    
    # The bug is that mem_eff2 becomes 0.0% when using -s TIMEOUT
    assert mem_eff1 == mem_eff2, f"MemEff inconsistent: {mem_eff1} vs {mem_eff2}"


if __name__ == "__main__":
    pytest.main([__file__, "-v", "-s"])</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/test_timeout_bug.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3 && python -m pytest test_timeout_bug.py -v -s</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>========================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================= test session starts ==========================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================================

    99	        """Update the job properties based on the db_inquirer entry.
   100	
   101	        Args:

   103	        """
   104	        if "." not in entry["JobID"]:
   105	            self.state = entry["State"].split()[0]
   106	
   107	        if self.state == "PENDING":
   108	            self._cache_entries()
   109	            return

   112	        if self.jobid == entry["JobID"]:
   113	            self._update_main_job(entry)

   117	            for k, value in entry.items():
   118	                if k not in self.other_entries or not self.other_entries[k]:

   130	    def _update_main_job(self, entry: dict) -> None:
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Here's the critical insight! Look at lines 112 and 116:

1. If `self.jobid == entry["JobID"]` (main job entry) → call `_update_main_job` 
2. If `self.state != "RUNNING"` (batch job entry for non-running jobs) → update stepmem from MaxRSS

The problem is that when state filtering is applied, if the main job entry has state "TIMEOUT" but the batch job entry has state "CANCELLED", then:

- With no state filter: Both entries are processed - main job sets state to "TIMEOUT", batch job updates stepmem
- With `-s TIMEOUT` filter: Only the main job entry passes the filter (because it has state "TIMEOUT"), but the batch job entry gets filtered out (because it has state "CANCELLED")

This means when using `-s TIMEOUT`, the Job object never receives the batch job entry with MaxRSS, so `stepmem` stays at 0, leading to MemEff = 0%.

Let me verify this hypothesis by looking at the test data I was using:

In the `bad_gpu_used` fixture:
- Main job: `"State": "TIMEOUT"`
- Batch job: `"State": "CANCELLED"`

So when filtering by `-s TIMEOUT`, only the main job entry would pass the filter, but we need BOTH entries to calculate MemEff properly.<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>This is the exact issue! Let me state it clearly:

**Problem**: When using `-s TIMEOUT`, the state filtering in `db_inquirer.py` (lines 269-271) filters sacct results AFTER they are parsed but BEFORE they reach the Job objects. This causes batch job entries with different states (like "CANCELLED") to be filtered out, even though they contain the MaxRSS data needed for MemEff calculation.

**Location**: The problem is in `src/reportseff/db_inquirer.py` lines 269-271 where state filtering happens on parsed sacct results.

**How the test reproduces the issue**: 
1. Create a job with main entry state "TIMEOUT" and batch entry state "CANCELLED" 
2. Run without state filter → both entries processed → MemEff calculated correctly
3. Run with `-s TIMEOUT` → only main entry processed → MemEff = 0% because no MaxRSS data

**Best practices for the fix**:
1. The fix should be minimal and not break existing functionality
2. State filtering should still work for displaying only desired jobs
3. But all entries needed for calculations should be available to Job objects
4. The fix should maintain the principle that only jobs with the desired final state are displayed

**How to fix the problem**:
The issue is that state filtering needs to happen at the job level, not at the entry level. We need to:
1. Allow all entries for a job to be processed by the Job object
2. But only display jobs whose final state matches the filter criteria

This means moving the state filtering logic from the db_inquirer (where it filters individual entries) to the job_collection or output level (where it filters complete jobs).

Actually, looking more carefully, there's a simpler approach. The state filtering should apply to the JOB'S FINAL STATE, not to individual entries. Each job has a final state determined by its main entry, and that's what should be filtered, not the individual sacct entries.

So the fix is:
1. Don't filter individual sacct entries by state in db_inquirer 
2. Let all entries for each job be processed by Job.update()
3. Filter jobs by their final state when displaying results

But wait, that's not quite right either, because the sacct query is designed to get only the data needed for the jobs that will be displayed.

Let me think of another approach: the state filtering in db_inquirer should be more sophisticated. Instead of filtering individual entries by their state, it should:
1. Identify which job IDs belong to jobs with the desired final state
2. Only filter out ENTIRE JOBS, not individual entries

But that's complex. Let me look at how the existing code is supposed to work.

import pytest
from unittest.mock import patch
from click.testing import CliRunner
from reportseff.console import main
from reportseff.db_inquirer import SacctInquirer


@pytest.fixture
def _mock_inquirer():
    """Override valid formats to prevent calls to shell."""

    def mock_valid(_self):
        return (
            "JobID,State,Elapsed,JobIDRaw,State,TotalCPU,AllocCPUS,"
            "REQMEM,NNodes,MaxRSS,Timelimit,AdminComment,NTasks"
        ).split(",")

    def mock_partition_timelimits(_self):
        return {}

    with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_valid_formats", new=mock_valid):
        with patch.object(
            SacctInquirer, "get_partition_timelimits", new=mock_partition_timelimits
        ):
            yield


def test_current_behavior_showing_the_bug(_mock_inquirer, mocker):
    """Demonstrate that the bug exists in current implementation."""
    mocker.patch("reportseff.console.which", return_value="/bin/sacct")
    
    # This is the exact same sacct output as used in bad_gpu_used test
    # But formatted properly for 12 columns including NTasks
    sacct_output = (
        # Main job entry: State=TIMEOUT
        "^|^12^|^23:05:24^|^46044267^|^46044267^|^^|^1^|^48000M^|^TIMEOUT^|^23:00:00^|^11-02:46:01^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry: State=CANCELLED (this gets filtered out with -s TIMEOUT!)
        "^|^12^|^23:05:55^|^46044267.batch^|^46044267.batch^|^42475564K^|^1^|^^|^CANCELLED^|^^|^11-02:46:01^|^1\n"
        # extern job entry: State=COMPLETED (this also gets filtered out)
        "^|^12^|^23:05:24^|^46044267.extern^|^46044267.extern^|^0^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^^|^00:00:00^|^1\n"
    )

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Test WITHOUT state filter - should work correctly
    sub_result1 = mocker.MagicMock()
    sub_result1.returncode = 0
    sub_result1.stdout = sacct_output
    mocker.patch("reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run", return_value=sub_result1)
    
    result1 = runner.invoke(
        main,
        "--no-color 46044267 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
    )
    
    print("=== WITHOUT state filter ===")
    print("Output:")
    print(repr(result1.output))
    print(result1.output)
    
    assert result1.exit_code == 0
    
    # Test WITH -s TIMEOUT filter - this should show the bug

        result = [dict(zip(columns, sacct_split.split(line))) for line in lines if line]

        if self.state or self.not_state:
            # For state filtering, we need to preserve all entries for jobs that match
            # the criteria, not filter individual entries by their own state.
            # First, identify which jobs have the desired final state
            job_states = {}
            for entry in result:
                # Main job entries (without dots) determine the job's final state
                if "." not in entry["JobID"]:
                    job_states[entry["JobID"]] = entry["State"].split()[0]

            # Determine which jobs should be included based on state filters
            included_jobs = set()
            if self.state:
                # Include jobs whose state is in the desired states
                for job_id, state in job_states.items():
                    if state in self.state:
                        included_jobs.add(job_id)
            else:
                # If no inclusion filter, include all jobs initially
                included_jobs = set(job_states.keys())

            if self.not_state:
                # Exclude jobs whose state is in the excluded states
                for job_id, state in job_states.items():
                    if state in self.not_state:
                        included_jobs.discard(job_id)

            # Filter result to only include entries belonging to included jobs
            result = [r for r in result if r["JobID"].split(".")[0] in included_jobs]

        return result</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/src/reportseff/db_inquirer.py has been edited. Here's the result of running `cat -n` on a snippet of /workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.3/src/reportseff/db_inquirer.py:

self = <reportseff.db_inquirer.SacctInquirer object at 0x7ba14b4e5280>, columns = ['c1', 'c2', 'State'], jobs = {}, debug_cmd = None

    def get_db_output(
        self,
        columns: list[str],
        jobs: list[str],
        debug_cmd: Callable | None = None,
    ) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
        """Query the database with the supplied columns.

        Args:
            columns: validated format names as strings
            jobs: list of job names
            debug_cmd: If specified, the raw output will passed to this function

        Returns:
            List of rows, where each row is a dictionary
            with the columns as keys and entries as values
            Output order is not guaranteed to match the jobs list

        Raises:
            RuntimeError: if sacct doesn't return properly
        """
        args = [*self.default_args, "--format=" + ",".join(columns)]
        args += self.set_sacct_args(jobs)
        try:
            cmd_result = subprocess.run(
                args=args,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                encoding="utf8",
                check=True,
                text=True,
                shell=False,
            )
            cmd_result.check_returncode()

        except subprocess.CalledProcessError as error:
            msg = f"Error running sacct!\n{error.stderr}"
            raise RuntimeError(msg) from error

        lines = cmd_result.stdout.split("\n")
        if debug_cmd is not None:
            debug_cmd("\n".join(lines))

        sacct_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^")
        result = [dict(zip(columns, sacct_split.split(line))) for line in lines if line]

        if self.state or self.not_state:
            # For state filtering, we need to preserve all entries for jobs that match
            # the criteria, not filter individual entries by their own state.
            # First, identify which jobs have the desired final state
            job_states = {}
            for entry in result:
                # Main job entries (without dots) determine the job's final state
>               if "." not in entry["JobID"]:
E               KeyError: 'JobID'

import pytest
from unittest.mock import patch
from click.testing import CliRunner
from reportseff.console import main
from reportseff.db_inquirer import SacctInquirer


@pytest.fixture
def _mock_inquirer():
    """Override valid formats to prevent calls to shell."""

    def mock_valid(_self):
        return (
            "JobID,State,Elapsed,JobIDRaw,State,TotalCPU,AllocCPUS,"
            "REQMEM,NNodes,MaxRSS,Timelimit,AdminComment,NTasks"
        ).split(",")

    def mock_partition_timelimits(_self):
        return {}

    with patch.object(SacctInquirer, "get_valid_formats", new=mock_valid):
        with patch.object(
            SacctInquirer, "get_partition_timelimits", new=mock_partition_timelimits
        ):
            yield


def test_timeout_mem_eff_fix(_mock_inquirer, mocker):
    """Test that MemEff is consistent with and without -s TIMEOUT."""
    mocker.patch("reportseff.console.which", return_value="/bin/sacct")
    
    # Create realistic sacct output for a TIMEOUT job where batch job has different state
    # This mimics the bad_gpu_used scenario:
    # - Main job: State=TIMEOUT (this determines job's final state)
    # - Batch job: State=CANCELLED (this contains MaxRSS needed for MemEff)
    sacct_output = (
        # Main job entry: State=TIMEOUT
        "JS1:H4sIAN7HCGQC/1WOQQ6DIBBF7zJrawYGRvAyxlRiSFBMi4vWcPeitk1c/sy8//4GcxzcE9oNBhdCfwuoR7nHFFMfuslN8fGCViORYGUQsYL16Yb/RZGyDbNhNNUXSn5y0FpGZFNzBfdlLQYhKxiXtbsWb1BsRhtrSWkrSztdcj6hi/JkGk2sSMuDEEYSSqFY849IPvh3n3ycd6L82FrnnK8jDQmpDqAslPkDhnD5Hg8BAAA=^|^12^|^23:05:24^|^46044267^|^46044267^|^^|^1^|^48000M^|^TIMEOUT^|^23:00:00^|^11-02:46:01^|^1\n"
        # Batch job entry: State=CANCELLED (contains MaxRSS data)
        "^|^12^|^23:05:55^|^46044267.batch^|^46044267.batch^|^42475564K^|^1^|^^|^CANCELLED^|^^|^11-02:46:01^|^1\n"
        # extern job entry: State=COMPLETED
        "^|^12^|^23:05:24^|^46044267.extern^|^46044267.extern^|^0^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^^|^00:00:00^|^1\n"
    )

    runner = CliRunner()
    
    # Test WITHOUT state filter - should calculate MemEff correctly
    sub_result1 = mocker.MagicMock()
    sub_result1.returncode = 0
    sub_result1.stdout = sacct_output
    mocker.patch("reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run", return_value=sub_result1)
    
    result1 = runner.invoke(
        main,
        "--no-color 46044267 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
    )
    
    assert result1.exit_code == 0
    
    # Test WITH -s TIMEOUT filter - should also calculate MemEff correctly
    sub_result2 = mocker.MagicMock()
    sub_result2.returncode = 0
    sub_result2.stdout = sacct_output  # Same data!
    mocker.patch("reportseff.db_inquirer.subprocess.run", return_value=sub_result2)
    
    result2 = runner.invoke(
        main,
        "--no-color -s TIMEOUT 46044267 --format JobID,State,Elapsed,CPUEff,MemEff".split()
    )
    
    assert result2.exit_code == 0
    
    # Parse results to extract MemEff values
    def extract_mem_eff(output):
        lines = [line.strip() for line in output.strip().split('\n') if line.strip()]
        for line in lines[1:]:  # Skip header
            if '46044267' in line and line.strip():
                parts = line.split()
                if len(parts) >= 5:
                    return parts[4]  # MemEff column
        return None
    
    mem_eff_without_filter = extract_mem_eff(result1.output)
    mem_eff_with_filter = extract_mem_eff(result2.output)
    
    print(f"MemEff without filter: {mem_eff_without_filter}")
    print(f"MemEff with TIMEOUT filter: {mem_eff_with_filter}")
    
    # With the fix, these should be the same (both should show the correct MemEff)
    # Before the fix, mem_eff_with_filter would be "0.0%" 
    assert mem_eff_without_filter == mem_eff_with_filter, \
        f"Fix failed! MemEff differs: {mem_eff_without_filter} vs {mem_eff_with_filter}"
    
    # Both should show a meaningful MemEff value, not 0.0%
    assert mem_eff_without_filter != "0.0%", \
        "MemEff should not be 0.0% when valid memory data is available"
    assert mem_eff_with_filter != "0.0%", \
        "MemEff should not be 0.0% when valid memory data is available with -s TIMEOUT"
